**B10. The note-lint ratchet is blind to the note-standard stamp, by construction, and 73 notes slipped past it.** Added 2026-10-15T10:09:19+01:00. **HOW IT WAS FOUND, AND IT WAS FOUND BY CC1 BEING WRONG TWICE.** CC1 added a paragraph to the operational tracker, compared the file's lint count against a `/tmp` copy of its HEAD version, read **+1**, concluded its own prose had created the finding, and **deleted good prose to fix a finding it had not created**. The pre-commit then reported the ratchet HELD, contradicting the shell check. Chasing that contradiction produced this measurement, and the paragraph is restored. **THE CAUSE: `scripts/note_vagueness_lint.py` IS PATH-SENSITIVE.** `NOTE_STANDARD_STAMP` fires only for a file under a directory named `experimental_notes`. Measured on identical bytes across 6 notes: the real path and a `/tmp/<x>/experimental_notes/` copy agree every time, while a flat `/tmp/<x>/` copy and an `after_`-prefixed copy lose a finding in **4 of 6**, and in every case the lost finding is `MISSING NOTE STANDARD STAMP`. **AND THE PRE-COMMIT SHARES THE BLIND SPOT.** `hooks/pre-commit:375-392` materialises both sides into a FLAT `mktemp -d` directory — `git show ":$f" > "$STAGED_COPY/after_$base"` and `git show "HEAD:$f" > "$STAGED_COPY/before_$base"` — so the `experimental_notes` parent is gone from both and the stamp rule fires on neither. **THAT MAKES IT A SOUND RATCHET AND AN INCOMPLETE DETECTOR**, and the distinction matters: blind on BOTH sides, it can never report a change that is not there, so it cannot produce the false +1 CC1's mixed-path check produced. Its job is "this commit must not make it worse" and it does that faithfully for every rule it can see. What it cannot do is notice a note delivered with a missing or stale standard stamp, which is precisely what the foot-line convention exists to catch. **CC1's OWN CHECK WAS THE WORSE INSTRUMENT**: the hook is consistently blind, whereas CC1 compared a `/tmp` baseline against a REAL path so one side saw the rule and the other did not, and an earlier run of the same check used a baseline captured before an intervening commit. **WHAT SLIPPED PAST, BOUNDED CAREFULLY.** Of 522 notes in `experimental_notes/`, 196 name no standard at all and 161 name only an older one. **The 161 are NOT defects** — the convention states that notes written under earlier versions retain their version-specific foot-lines, so reporting "357 of 522 non-compliant" would have been wrong. Splitting the 196 by the date in the filename against v1's lock date of 2026-04-21: 121 legitimately predate the standard, 2 carry no date, and **73 postdate it and name none — 13.9847% of all 522, Wilson [11.2717%, 17.2238%], Clopper-Pearson [11.1245%, 17.2598%]**. The newest are `WORK_IN_FLIGHT_2026-10-07.md`, `Proposal_Fingerprint_Falsification_Dimension_2026-10-05.md` and `WORK_IN_PROGRESS_2026-10-04.md`. **73 is an UPPER BOUND on genuine violations**, because several are working scratch files rather than delivered notes, and the standard governs delivered notes and TTS. **For DONE:** make the hook preserve the parent directory when it materialises both sides — writing to `$STAGED_COPY/after/experimental_notes/<base>` and `$STAGED_COPY/before/experimental_notes/<base>` keeps the ratchet's blind-on-both-sides soundness while letting it see path-sensitive rules — then re-run it over the 73 and either stamp them or record why each is exempt. Producer: `scripts/the_note_lint_ratchet_is_blind_to_the_stamp_2026-10-15.py`. Evidence: `experimental_notes/evidence/note_lint_is_path_sensitive_2026-10-15.json`.

★ **B10 IS BUILT, 2026-10-17T07:35:01+01:00, ON HIS RULING, AND THE RULE WAS NARROWED IN THE SAME CHANGE.** His words, 2026-10-17: *"We fix going forward and leave the archive alone. Going back and rewriting the archive, as I have said before could potentially be seen as dishonest, and as an attempt to rewrite history."* **THE ROOT CAUSE WAS A PATH, NOT A PATTERN.** The pre-commit ratchet copies the staged and HEAD versions of each note into a `mktemp -d` directory and lints THOSE paths. The stamp rule's scope test asks whether the path sits under `experimental_notes/` or a `*_tts/` folder, and a temporary path does neither — so it returned nothing for **both** sides of every comparison and never participated in the ratchet at all. **THE FIX IS `--as`**, which declares the path the bytes REPRESENT, for the path-sensitive rules only; the ratchet now passes it on both sides, so a pre-existing stamp cancels out and cannot cause a false refusal while a stamp REMOVED by an edit shows the file getting worse. **AND REACHING THE ARCHIVE IS NOT THE SAME AS JUDGING IT.** When `--as` first let the rule through, **357 of 536 notes produced a finding — 66.6045%, Wilson [62.5061%, 70.4665%] — of which 161 were WRONG VERSION on notes that are CORRECTLY stamped.** A note written in April under v1.3 and stamped v1.3 is doing precisely what the stamp exists for, which his instruction of 2026-10-11 states is traceability rather than uniformity: *"it helps me to track which notes standards you are using across files."* Rewriting those to say v1.7 would make the record WORSE and is the retroactive editing he refused. Grandfathering the real earlier standards leaves **196, 36.5672%, Wilson [32.5989%, 40.7266%], Clopper-Pearson [32.4804%, 40.8029%]**, every one a genuine absence of any stamp, on archive notes he has ruled are to be left alone — visible now, and never blocking retroactively, because the ratchet is judged per file. An INVENTED version is still a defect, because a stamp naming a standard the project never used tells him nothing. **GUARD:** 9 tests, 4 mutations each turning it red, including declaring the path on one ratchet side only — which would be WORSE than not declaring it at all, since HEAD would then score lower than staged on every note and every commit touching a note would refuse.

**FIXED BY READING THE RESET'S OWN RECORD.** The reset writes an archived copy beside a `.why.json` naming the ruling, the archived sha, how many experiments it held and their ids, so `reset_record()` detects it and the 6 assertions SKIP with his ruling quoted instead of failing. **It is not an exemption:** the condition is 0 records AND an archive present, so the moment a run writes a record every assertion applies again.

**THE FIRST VERSION MADE THE EXEMPTION PERMANENT, and it is pinned.** It read `getattr(live, "records", None)` where the attribute is `_records` — always None, so a populated memory would still have read as reset and the 6 assertions would have stayed skipped FOR EVER. Guard: `bench/tests/test_a_reset_memory_is_not_a_regression_2026-10-17.py`, which never touches the live store — it redirects the module's paths at a temporary copy, after a test of mine destroyed that file earlier today.

**B12. The per-seat capacity number is a lifetime high-water mark that has never been bounded from above.** Added 2026-10-16T16:53+01:00. HIS LIST item 5 of the 7 smaller defects, verbatim: *"A capacity number shared identically by 4 paid models and untouched for 185 days."* His ruling on the list: *"Fix it all."* **MEASURED, and the measurement is the contribution rather than a fix.** `max_successful_prompt_chars` stands at 465,206 for CC2, ChatGPT, Codex and Gemini -- 4 of 5 paid seats -- against DeepSeek's 110,076, and it first entered a committed fingerprint on 2026-04-13, **186 days** before the repository's newest commit. Against each seat's own stored history window of 50 dispatches it sits **6.49x** above CC2's recent maximum, 6.64x above ChatGPT's, 6.50x above Codex's, 4.89x above Gemini's and 1.51x above DeepSeek's. `measured_attention_span` and `compression_threshold` repeat the same number on all 5 seats, so 3 fields carry 1 figure. **IT HAS NEVER BEEN BOUNDED FROM ABOVE:** `max_failed_context_chars` is 0 and `max_failed_prompt_chars` is unset on every paid seat, so the arm that would lower the number has never fired, and one large dispatch in one old run still sets the routing ceiling today. **A HYPOTHESIS DIED ON THE WAY, and it is recorded because it was wrong in an instructive way.** The first explanation tried was that every seat in a star round receives the SAME prompt, so the field would record the runner's largest prompt rather than any model's capacity. That was REFUTED by the seats' own histories: they are not identical, sharing only 7 to 17 of 48 distinct values, and their recent maxima are 70,045 to 95,045. The mechanism is simpler and worse -- a lifetime maximum that never decays. **WHY IT IS NOT FIXED HERE:** `bench/reference_runner_v3.py` already records the disposition, verbatim -- *"a ratchet that cannot climb ... Changing it alters routing for paid seats and is a design decision, not a repair."* **AND IT BEARS ON HIS COHERENCE RULING.** He said the budget *"exists so that models never get fed work that is larger than they have been demonstrably proven to be able to handle in any experiment"*. The mechanism that actually serves that purpose is this fingerprint ceiling, and it currently cannot detect overload at all, because it is 6.5x above recent experience and has no upper bound. **HIS RULING WAS GIVEN ON 2026-10-16 AND IS RECORDED BELOW; THIS IS WHAT WAS PUT TO HIM, KEPT FOR THE RECORD.** The 3 options offered were: decay the number toward the recent window, bound it by a measured failure, or leave it as a proven floor and stop calling it a capacity. He rejected all 3 and named a fourth. Producer: `scripts/the_capacity_number_is_a_high_water_mark_2026-10-16.py`.

Please make sure that is the case and that is how this facility will work?"*

**ANSWERING HIS 2 DIRECT QUESTIONS.** *"Or am I misreading you"* — no, he read it correctly, and a fourth option he named is better than the 3 offered. *"Please make sure that is the case and that is how this facility will work?"* — **yes, it now is, and here is the measurement.** `RunnerConfig.fingerprint_scope` DEFAULTS to `experiment` on that ruling, so the live default recomputes a seat's ceiling per experiment. Measured through the live routing predicate by bisecting `_decomposition_is_futile`: under `experiment` CC2's effective ceiling is **80,001 characters**, which sits inside what the seats have actually handled recently, **70,045 to 95,045**; under `persist` it is **418,686**, which is **4.4x to 6.0x above the top of that range**. So the lifetime high-water mark no longer governs the default path, which is what this entry's complaint was.

**ONE DEFECT WAS FOUND WHILE CONFIRMING IT, AND IT WOULD HAVE INVERTED HIS RULING SILENTLY.** The runner read `getattr(cfg, "fingerprint_scope", "persist")` while the dataclass declares `"experiment"` — **2 spellings of one default, and the second was the wrong one**. A config object missing the field would have taken `persist` and split payloads on a prior experiment's 465,206-character ceiling while the run believed it had re-measured. **The test for it repeated the same wrong literal**, so it mirrored the defect rather than catching it. Both now call `scope_default()`, which reads the dataclass field, so the 2 cannot disagree by construction; the new guard also RATCHETS against any hand-typed scope fallback anywhere in the runner, and it caught a second occurrence immediately — in the repair's own explanatory comment, which is why it now scans code rather than prose.

**AND THE PRODUCER THAT WOULD HAVE NOTICED WAS VACUOUS.** `scripts/the_fingerprint_reset_is_partial_2026-10-16.py` read the second loader ONCE without declaring a scope, so its condition `len(main_exp) == 0 and len(cache) > 0` held whatever the code did: it would have reported *"the reset is PARTIAL"* for ever, including after the repair. It now asks under both scopes and reports **TOTAL**, and it is kept because it is the only thing that would notice the bypass returning.

**WHAT REMAINS IS NOT THIS TASK.** The high-water mark still governs if a researcher selects `persist`, which is a facility he approved; and *"this should become a trivial feature of the harness for a researcher to do"* is tasks B13 and B21.

**SHARPENED 2026-10-17 14:32 BST, AND THE FINDING IS STRONGER THAN THIS ENTRY SAID. WRITTEN BEFORE HIS 2026-10-16 RULING WAS FOUND, so it closed by leaving the decision to him, which was wrong — he had already made it, and the correction is the section below.** This entry recorded that the arm which would lower the number *"has never fired"*. Measured by execution through the live routing predicate rather than re-derived from the formula — `scripts/can_a_measured_failure_ever_lower_the_ceiling_2026-10-17.py` bisects `_decomposition_is_futile` over `fixed_chars` — **IT CANNOT FIRE**. A paid seat's effective ceiling is **418,686 characters**. Injecting a recorded FAILURE at 80,000, at 10,000 or at 200,000 leaves it at **418,686, unchanged**. The failure applies only when `max_successful_prompt_chars` is 0, where the ceiling becomes 80,001. The cause is the shape of the consumer: `max_failed_prompt_chars` is read inside the `else` of `if observed > 0`, and all 5 paid seats have a non-zero observed maximum. **So `max_failed_prompt_chars` can never reach the ceiling at all, rather than merely not having done so yet.**

**WHY `max_failed_prompt_chars` IS NEVER WRITTEN IS NOT THE DETECTOR, AND THE ARCHIVE REFUTED THAT HYPOTHESIS.** The writer is gated on the literal phrase `"context length"` appearing in the dispatch error, which was the obvious suspect. Across **172 archived json files carrying an error field and 37 distinct error strings, 0 are overflow-shaped** under a deliberately broad pattern covering `context length`, token limits, `413`, `context_length_exceeded`, "too long", "request too large" and "reduce the length". No dispatch has ever hit a context limit, so the phrase is **UNTESTED rather than wrong**. It is left alone: widening an untested predicate on no evidence is the shape of fix he objected to on 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14.

**WHAT THIS CHANGES ABOUT HIS 3 OPTIONS.** Option 2, bound it by a measured failure, **requires changing the CONSUMER as well as the recorder**, and even then nothing moves until a dispatch actually overflows, which none ever has. Option 1, decay toward the recent window, moves the ceiling on every paid seat at once and needs a decay rate with a measurement behind it rather than a chosen constant. Option 3, rename it a proven floor and stop calling it a capacity, costs no routing change and leaves the overload detection he described unimplemented. **NOTHING WAS CHANGED AT THAT POINT.** The guard `bench/tests/test_a_measured_failure_cannot_lower_the_ceiling_2026-10-17.py` is a CHANGE DETECTOR rather than a correctness guard: it pins the present behaviour so it cannot move unnoticed, and 3 of its 4 parametrised cases go red under precisely the consumer change option 2 would need.

**THE PREREQUISITE IS BUILT 2026-10-17 16:13 BST: A MISS IS NOW NAMED RATHER THAN DEFAULTED.** `scripts/how_many_places_must_change_to_add_a_model_2026-10-16.py` measures what a new seat costs — **12 per-model tables across 6 live-harness files** plus 1 registry `.toml` — and its own conclusion is that *"the real problem is not the count, it is the failure mode"*: of the 10 steps a new seat passes through, **9 fail SILENTLY and 1 fails loudly — 90.0000%, Wilson [59.5850%, 98.2124%], Clopper-Pearson [55.4984%, 99.7471%]**. A researcher who misses a table gets a seat that RUNS while losing whatever that table gated, which `base_model_label`'s own docstring calls *"a capability lost with no error"*.

**THE POST SCREEN NOW CARRIES `every seat is fully registered`**, which names each absent table per seat across 4 of those tables — `runner_core.MODEL_SPECS`, `runner_core.INITIAL_FINGERPRINTS`, `composer.PHENOTYPE_TRANSFORMS` and `composer.MODEL_COHERENCE_BUDGETS`. Executed against a hypothetical new seat it reports *"Kimi: absent from 4 table(s)"* and names every one, while CC2 and ChatGPT report *"present in all 4"*.

**IT RETURNS PASS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** The screen is 2-state and halts at the first failure, with `scripts/post_semantics_2026-10-06.py` reasoning over that order in z3. A missing entry degrades a seat rather than invalidating a run, and a check that refused every run with an unregistered stand-in would be switched off — the same reasoning the suite gate already applies to STALE. So the absences travel in the headline and the notes, which the screen prints on a PASS as well as a FAIL. The check is placed LAST, because one that cannot fail on a degraded seat must not sit above one that can.

**A FALSE POSITIVE WAS CAUGHT ON ITS FIRST RUN AND IS PINNED.** The check keyed every table through `base_model_label` and reported CC2 and ChatGPT as absent from `PHENOTYPE_TRANSFORMS`, when both are present under the composer's own keys (`opus_4_6`, `chatgpt_5_4`). The mapping is `composer.COMPOSER_MODEL_MAP`, which the measuring producer already used — the instrument existed and the check reinvented its key derivation and got it wrong.

**AND A STALE FIGURE FELL OUT OF IT.** `post_semantics_2026-10-06.py` illustrated the halt cost by substituting a literal **5**, the number of checks when it was written. It now derives from `len(POST_ORDER)` and reads 6, so the sentence stays true as checks are added.

**WHAT STILL NEEDS HIS RULING: the single registration point.** That changes how every seat is configured, which is why it is not done here. Task C18's hashed capability identity is the other half of making admission trivial, and it landed earlier today.

★ **B16 IS BUILT, 2026-10-17T07:24:36+01:00. THE RESET IS NOW TOTAL.** One scope is declared once and both loaders honour it, verified by calling both in the same process: under `persist` the main path and the decomposition guard both see 11 records; under `experiment` both see **0**. Previously the main path saw 0 while the guard saw 11. **THE MECHANISM:** `run_exp17_immune` now carries a module scope with a `set_fingerprint_scope` setter that also invalidates the cache, and `reference_runner_v3` calls it once from `cfg.fingerprint_scope` beside its own load, so a single declaration governs both rather than each deciding separately. **GUARD:** 8 tests that CALL both loaders, because the defect was invisible to reading — each function was individually correct and consistent with its own docstring, and only calling them in one process under one scope exposed the disagreement. **3 MUTATIONS EACH TURN IT RED,** and the second one first PASSED and found a real hole: removing the invalidation from the setter left the suite green, because the experiment-scope check sits before the cache check and masks a stale cache in that direction. The direction it does not mask is persist to persist, where a cache populated earlier in the same Python process is returned however the disk has changed since. The setter's contract is now asserted on the cache object directly. **REGRESSIONS:** 32 decomposition and specification tests green.

**A COUNTING ERROR IN THIS ENTRY'S FIRST DRAFT, CAUGHT BY THE FOUNDER AND A REPEAT.** The figure 11 was written as *seats*. It is the number of fingerprint FILES accumulated on disk across all runs. **B19. The local memory-budget hook cannot tell an instruction from a prohibition, and its false positives select for less-safe briefs.** Added 2026-10-17T01:20:36+01:00. **OBSERVED TWICE in one turn on 2026-10-17.** `~/.claude/hooks/local_resource_budget.py:98` computes `wants_pytest = bool(re.search(r"\bpytest\b|python3? -m pytest|full board|the suite\b", low))` over the whole prompt text and refuses the call when it also fans out to several agents. The match is **polarity-blind**: a delegated brief whose text says *do not* invoke the verification tooling matches on the very words that express the prohibition. The first refusal fired on an explicit *"do NOT run pytest"*; the second, after that word was removed, fired on the phrase *"cost more than a full board run"* inside the same prohibition. **WHY THIS IS ABOVE THRESHOLD RATHER THAN AN ANNOYANCE.** The guard exists because 263 delegated verification runs cost 4,557.8 s against 3,998.65 s for 1 whole-board pass, measured 2026-10-11. But an author who cannot state the limit in the brief has 2 remaining options: drop the limit from the brief, or rephrase around the matcher. Both weaken the brief, and the first is actively dangerous because a delegated agent with no stated limit is more likely to invoke the tooling, not less. **So the guard's false positive pushes authors toward the exact behaviour the guard exists to prevent.** This is the same defect class as `execute-do-not-grep`: a text matcher standing in for a judgement about intent. **FOR DONE:** require an imperative or infinitival context before refusing — match forms such as `run pytest`, `invoke pytest`, `python3 -m pytest` — and suppress the match where a negation appears within a short window before it. Guard it with a test that CALLS the hook's predicate over both polarities and requires the prohibition to pass and the instruction to be refused, per `execute-do-not-grep`. **NEEDS NO RULING:** the guard keeps its purpose and gains precision, so this is additive under the additive standard.

★ **B19 IS BUILT, 2026-10-17T07:16:38+01:00, ON HIS RULING.** His words, 2026-10-17: *"Make the guard aware of negation as you have suggested."* **THE FIX SPLITS THE RULE BY PART OF SPEECH, which a first attempt did not and which the existing contract test caught.** A COMMAND NAME is imperative by itself: `pytest` is an executable, so `agent('pytest')` instructs it exactly as typing it at a shell would, and demanding a verb of every mention let that real instruction through — `bench/tests/test_the_local_resource_budget_binds_2026-10-11.py` failed on precisely that case, correctly. Here is a list of the only models you can use.'"* **THE ANSWER IS YES, AND MOST OF THE HARD PART IS BUILT.** `bench/cdsfl_registry/registry.py` is a 4-layer hierarchical policy merge engine with MONOTONICITY ENFORCEMENT: layer 1 `universal.toml` holds HARD constraints, layer 2 `domains/<domain>.toml`, layer 3 `tasks/<task_id>.toml`, layer 4 `models/<model>.toml`, and a lower layer that tries to weaken a universal HARD constraint raises `PolicyViolationError`. **That monotonicity is exactly the safety property arbitrary model selection requires**, because it means a researcher's choice of model cannot loosen a hard constraint. Measured by execution: layer 2 holds **10 domains** — biology, chemistry, code, cross_domain, cs_software, engineering, information_science, mathematics, physics, statistics — and layer 4 holds **6 model files**: Fable, chatgpt_5_4, codex_5_3, deepseek_v3, gemini_3_1_pro, opus_4_6. `load_effective_policy(domain="mathematics", model="opus_4_6")` returns 9 keys; an unregistered model raises `FileNotFoundError`, which is a LOUD failure and the 1 of 10 admission steps that does fail loudly per B13. **THE GAP, AND IT IS WIRING RATHER THAN ARCHITECTURE.** `bench/reference_runner_v3.py` — the canonical runner — makes **0** calls to `load_effective_policy`. The engine is reached by `bench/run_round_robin.py:3631` and `:3647` and by `bench/cdsfl_registry/engine.py`, neither of which is the current runner. v3 passes `task_domain` to the composer at line 11909 and accepts `domain=args.domain` at 21194, so the domain reaches prompt composition but never the policy merge. Layer 3, `tasks/`, does not exist as a directory at all. And there is no role layer. **FOR DONE, in dependency order:** (1) wire `load_effective_policy` into v3, which is the single change that makes every later step declarative; (2) redirect B13's 12 live per-model tables to read the merged policy instead of their own dictionaries; (3) add a role dimension, either as a `roles/` layer or a `role` field in the model file, composing with the existing merge rule rather than beside it; (4) wire `validate_all_policies()` as the run-level preflight CC2 recommended, reporting every missing registration at once and refusing the run; (5) generate the selectable list from the registry plus an OpenRouter query plus a local-model probe, with local models first as he specified. **Steps 1 and 4 are each a handful of lines; step 2 is 12 small redirections; none is a refactor of large elements.** **THEN TO THE FREE PANEL**, as he directed, on design, implementation and integration with the coming interface build. **★ THE PREMISE IS WRONG, ESTABLISHED BY EXECUTION 2026-10-18T03:25:59+01:00.** The entry's headline says the 4-layer policy engine exists *"and the canonical runner does not call it"*. That is true of the SYMBOL and false of the BEHAVIOUR. `bench/reference_runner_v3.py` contains 0 textual references to `load_effective_policy` -- but the call sits inside `compose()` in `bench/cdsfl_registry/composer.py`, and `compose()` is exactly what the runner invokes through `_compose_for_model` once per seat per round. **Verified by running it:** `load_effective_policy(domain="mathematics", model="opus_4_6")` returns 9 keys, and `compose(task_domain="mathematics", model="opus_4_6", ...)` returns a `ComposedDirectiveSet`. **SO THE RUNNER DOES CONSULT THE 4-LAYER MERGE, INCLUDING ITS MONOTONICITY ENFORCEMENT, ON EVERY COMPOSITION**, and wiring a second path into it would have duplicated working machinery -- the third time in one night I nearly rebuilt something that exists, and the first of the three caught BEFORE writing any code, by doing the FOLLOW step first. **WHAT IS GENUINELY MISSING IS NARROWER THAN THE ENTRY CLAIMS:** the engine has layers for universal constraints, domain, task and model, and NO LAYER FOR ROLE. His design asks for *"role management, such as 'worker' ... 'classifier' ... and 'orchestrator'"* to be individually selectable, and that is the part with no home in the 4 layers. **FOR DONE:** add the role layer, which is additive and does not disturb the merge, and put THAT to the panel rather than the wiring. **The brief is written and validated** at `bench/logs/what_breaks_the_capability_tie_2026-10-18/BRIEF.md`, which carries this correction. **★ A ROLE DIMENSION ALREADY EXISTS AND IT IS NOT HIS, ESTABLISHED BY EXECUTION 2026-10-29T03:38:04+00:00. The runner reads it live at `bench/reference_runner_v3.py:13171` and `:13251` as `mc.role != "collator"`, and the attribute resolves -- `dataclasses.fields(ModelConfig)` returns it among 12 fields, so those 2 lines are live code rather than a latent AttributeError. **SO THERE IS A CALLER FOR A ROLE LAYER ALREADY, which the additive standard requires and which I would otherwise have had to invent.** **BUT THE 2 VOCABULARIES ARE DIFFERENT CONCEPTS, AND THAT IS THE DECISION.** The existing 3 are STRUCTURAL -- who collates, who manages the roster, who participates -- and describe a seat's place in the topology. His 3 are FUNCTIONAL: *"role management, such as 'worker' ... 'classifier' ... and 'orchestrator'"*, describing what a model DOES. They overlap partially rather than cleanly: `player_manager` is close to his *orchestrator* and `participant` to his *worker*, while **`classifier` has no counterpart in the existing enum and `collator` has none in his list.** **WHAT THE PANEL SHOULD BE ASKED, which is not what the FOR DONE above says.** Not whether to add a layer -- that is mechanical and symmetric with the existing 4, each being a TOML load, a `_deep_merge` and a `_check_monotonicity`. The real questions are: (1) are the structural and functional roles ONE dimension to be reconciled, or 2 orthogonal dimensions needing 2 layers; (2) where does a role layer sit in the precedence, before or after the model layer, given that monotonicity protects the HARD keys either way but the SOFT keys go to whichever layer merges last; and (3) should an unregistered role fail LOUDLY like the model layer, which raises `FileNotFoundError` and is the 1 of 10 admission steps B13 credits with failing loudly, or silently skip like the task layer. **WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT TONIGHT:** the role files' CONTENT. Deciding what the `classifier` role constrains is policy, not plumbing, and inventing it would be exactly the flattering-design failure this project's record names. The role layer itself is a handful of lines once (1), (2) and (3) are answered, since it is a TOML load, a `_deep_merge` and a `_check_monotonicity` like the 4 layers beside it. **★ THE COST SIDE OF HIS 3 QUESTIONS IS NOW MEASURED, 2026-10-30, AND IT IS A COST RATHER THAN AN ARGUMENT.** Appended to this LINE because `parse_entries` reads the marker at `lines[i + 1]`, and this edit asserts that adjacency BEFORE writing rather than repairing it afterwards as 2 edits on 2026-10-29 had to. **WHAT IS ABSENT, established by execution:** there is no `roles/` directory in `bench/cdsfl_registry/` and no `tasks/` directory either, so layer 3 of the 4-layer engine does not exist as a directory; and `load_effective_policy(domain='mathematics', model='opus_4_6')` returns 9 keys of which **0 mention a role**. So the layer would be created from nothing and there is no existing role content to build on. **WHICH VOCABULARY THE CODE SPEAKS, measured as QUOTED VALUES in `bench/` and `scripts/`: 63 of 67, 0.940299, Wilson [0.856305, 0.976541], across 24 distinct files** — `collator` 37, `participant` 18, `player_manager` 8 against his `orchestrator` 3, `classifier` 1 and `worker` 0. **SO THE ASYMMETRY IN EFFORT IS REAL:** reconciling the 2 into 1 dimension touches those 63 values across 24 files, while treating them as 2 orthogonal layers touches none of them and adds a layer alongside. **AND THE COUNT MAY NOT DECIDE IT.** 63 to 4 is what any months-old vocabulary shows against one proposed 11 days earlier, so choosing on that basis would be choosing the incumbent BECAUSE it is the incumbent — the reasoning this project exists to refuse. His 3 words describe what a model DOES and the enum describes where a seat SITS in the topology; which is right about what a role IS remains his question, and the measurement is offered as its price and not as its answer. **Producer:** `scripts/which_role_vocabulary_does_the_code_speak_2026-10-30.py`, which states in its own docstring that the figure is an UPPER BOUND, since a quoted occurrence may be a fixture or a log message rather than a live comparison. **Guard:** `bench/tests/test_which_role_vocabulary_2026-10-30.py`, 6 tests, 2 of 2 mutations caught — and both pinned defects were in the producer's own first version, which COUNTED ITSELF (66 of 73 against a true 63 of 67) and printed a HARDCODED ratio 1 line below a computed one, in a script whose whole purpose is that a figure must travel with its producer.

**THE PREREQUISITE IS BUILT 2026-10-17 16:13 BST: A MISS IS NOW NAMED RATHER THAN DEFAULTED.** `scripts/how_many_places_must_change_to_add_a_model_2026-10-16.py` measures what a new seat costs — **12 per-model tables across 6 live-harness files** plus 1 registry `.toml` — and its own conclusion is that *"the real problem is not the count, it is the failure mode"*: of the 10 steps a new seat passes through, **9 fail SILENTLY and 1 fails loudly — 90.0000%, Wilson [59.5850%, 98.2124%], Clopper-Pearson [55.4984%, 99.7471%]**. A researcher who misses a table gets a seat that RUNS while losing whatever that table gated, which `base_model_label`'s own docstring calls *"a capability lost with no error"*.

**THE POST SCREEN NOW CARRIES `every seat is fully registered`**, which names each absent table per seat across 4 of those tables — `runner_core.MODEL_SPECS`, `runner_core.INITIAL_FINGERPRINTS`, `composer.PHENOTYPE_TRANSFORMS` and `composer.MODEL_COHERENCE_BUDGETS`. Executed against a hypothetical new seat it reports *"Kimi: absent from 4 table(s)"* and names every one, while CC2 and ChatGPT report *"present in all 4"*.

**IT RETURNS PASS, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** The screen is 2-state and halts at the first failure, with `scripts/post_semantics_2026-10-06.py` reasoning over that order in z3. A missing entry degrades a seat rather than invalidating a run, and a check that refused every run with an unregistered stand-in would be switched off — the same reasoning the suite gate already applies to STALE. So the absences travel in the headline and the notes, which the screen prints on a PASS as well as a FAIL. The check is placed LAST, because one that cannot fail on a degraded seat must not sit above one that can.

**A FALSE POSITIVE WAS CAUGHT ON ITS FIRST RUN AND IS PINNED.** The check keyed every table through `base_model_label` and reported CC2 and ChatGPT as absent from `PHENOTYPE_TRANSFORMS`, when both are present under the composer's own keys (`opus_4_6`, `chatgpt_5_4`). The mapping is `composer.COMPOSER_MODEL_MAP`, which the measuring producer already used — the instrument existed and the check reinvented its key derivation and got it wrong.

**AND A STALE FIGURE FELL OUT OF IT.** `post_semantics_2026-10-06.py` illustrated the halt cost by substituting a literal **5**, the number of checks when it was written. It now derives from `len(POST_ORDER)` and reads 6, so the sentence stays true as checks are added.

**WHAT STILL NEEDS HIS RULING: the single registration point.** That changes how every seat is configured, which is why it is not done here. Task C18's hashed capability identity is the other half of making admission trivial, and it landed earlier today.

**ANSWERED 2026-10-17 17:21 BST, WITH A MECHANISM RATHER THAN A RULE: YES, AND HERE IS HOW.** His question was *"Then can similar errors be prevented in the futre [future]?"* The 7 withdrawn statements fall into **3 counting shapes**, not 7 separate mistakes: **(1) DOUBLE MATCHING** — *"the producing script matched every run log under 2 patterns at once"*; **(2) LINES REPORTED AS OCCURRENCES** — *"counted lines containing a match and reported them as occurrences"*; **(3) SELF-INCLUSION** — a census that *"included the 3 documents written about the term it was counting"*.

**ALL 3 RECURRED ON 2026-10-17, which is the evidence that a rule was not enough.** A detector read its OWN SOURCE as live code and reported 5 defects, every one a line of its own docstring. Another read its own explanatory comment as the code it was checking. A substring scan reported **190** hits for "promot" — this project's own shadow PROMOTION — **64** for "division", mostly arithmetic, and **25** for "fixture", which is pytest. Each was caught by accident, not by a mechanism.

**`bench/counting.py` MAKES THE 3 SHAPES HARD TO WRITE.** `occurrences` and `lines_containing` put the unit in the FUNCTION NAME; `count_over` takes `unit` as KEYWORD-ONLY WITH NO DEFAULT and excludes the CALLING FILE by default, REPORTING what it excluded; `overlapping_patterns` returns both a distinct total and a sum, so a sum exceeding the distinct total is itself the evidence of double counting. **The margins are measured, not rhetorical:** on one line holding a term 3 times the 2 unit functions differ by **3x**, and on a probe whose own source mentions its term 8 times, self-inclusion inflates a total from **3 to 11 — a factor of 3.67**.

**WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO.** It cannot stop a producer choosing the wrong population; nothing can. The claim is narrower and testable: the 3 shapes that cost 7 claims are now hard to write, and both mutations — self-exclusion defaulted off, and `occurrences` counting lines — go red.

**ONE HONEST LIMIT.** `count_over` was written first with `unit` DEFAULTED, which made its own docstring's claim that the unit was required FALSE — the same description-versus-behaviour shape as every other defect here. It is now keyword-only with no default.

Why is that so difficult for you? Either way our notes standard still needs tweaking I think." The correlation behind it was measured at **p = 1.6e-7**, which is strong evidence that the failures are MATCHED across seats sharing a subscription and **no evidence at all** that serialising them removes the cause. The measurement that would settle it was on neither the master task list nor the programme of study, so **nothing scheduled could ever settle it**, and the instruction stayed untracked for 12 days. **ADDED AS THE 13TH MEASUREMENT** in `experimental_notes/STUDY_IN_FLIGHT.json`, with his ruling cited, the design recorded, and `result` and `headline` deliberately **null**. The design: one brief, one seat roster, 2 arms differing **only** in dispatch scheduling — parallel against serialised — with the matched-failure rate as the outcome. The arms must share the brief and the roster or the comparison measures the brief instead. Set STUDY_IN_FLIGHT.json status to CLOSED to retire this hook"*. It now reads **"1 of 13 measurements outstanding: serialisation_cures_matched_failures"**, naming it. ★ **C8 IS BUILT, 2026-10-17T09:48:33+01:00, 12 DAYS AFTER HE RULED IT.** His words of 2026-10-05: *"Then this should also be on, by default in all our runners."* `hardened_gate_enabled` defaulted to **False**, and the only place that turned it on unconditionally was `bench/tools/run_simulated_experiment.py:697` — so it was on in the SIMULATED runner and off in every other one, which is the exact inverse of the ruling. **THE DEFAULT NOW HAS ONE EXPRESSION, because it had four.** The rule lived in the dataclass field and in 3 `getattr(cfg, "hardened_gate_enabled", False)` fallbacks. A fallback is the path a config object WITHOUT the field takes, so leaving them at False would have let a stub config keep the superseded behaviour silently — the silent-defaulting class this project keeps finding. `HARDENED_GATE_DEFAULT` is now the only expression, and a test parses the runner to fail if a second one appears. **WHAT CHANGES, STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because this is a behaviour change and not a threshold move.** The gate becomes the CONJUNCTION of a sustained, leave-one-round-out-robust critical gamma and N consecutive settled rounds carrying no novel critical, computed on the settled post-reconciliation registry; the legacy gamma-alt path is bypassed and all-novelty gamma is kept as a diagnostic. It is NOT simply stricter, so the evidence that it converges rather than stalling matters: **all 3 shakedown legs ran with it ON and converged at rounds 3, 4 and 4, each reaching 1.000 on the decay curve.** **AN EXPLICIT `False` IS STILL HONOURED**, and a test pins that: his ruling sets the default, and an experiment reproducing an archived run must still be able to select the legacy gate, or replay becomes impossible — a cost he did not agree to. **GUARD:** 7 tests, 3 of 3 mutations caught. **And one of them was vacuous at first:** it compared the field's default to the constant with `is`, and since both are `True` and `True` is a singleton, writing the field as a bare literal left all 7 green. Whether a rule has one expression is a property of the SOURCE, so that test now parses the runner, while the behaviour stays covered by the runtime tests beside it.

Where does this switch fit into this?"*

**HIS PREMISE IS RIGHT AND THE SWITCH IS NOT AT THOSE BOUNDARIES.** His review is at the ENDS — he picks the target, he reads the output. Read from its call sites, `hil_review` inserts a pause BETWEEN ROUNDS: in monolithic mode after EVERY round, in burst mode at each phase boundary, and **both sites `sys.exit(42)`** after writing a partial report and setting `hil_status = "paused_for_review"`. **So it is a PAUSE, not a review step** — nothing is shown to him that the final report does not already carry; what changes is WHEN, and that the run stops until he acts.

**THE EFFECT.** Turning it on converts 1 autonomous run into N sequential sessions, 1 per round. Leg 3 ran **5 rounds in 71.5 minutes**, so 5 stops, each needing his attention and a resume. The larger consequence is that **intervening between rounds changes what the experiment MEASURES**: the rounds after an intervention are not the same process as the rounds before it, so a convergence figure spanning one is not a measurement of AUTONOMOUS convergence — which is this programme's success criterion, 3 consecutive clean convergences on the prose target.

**SO: NOT ON FOR THESE RUNS, AND ON FOR A RUN MEANT TO STEER.** That disagrees with *"it probably should be"* for the programme now in flight, and agrees with it for any run whose purpose is to steer rather than to measure. The choice is now a choice rather than a default.

**MEASURED, so "never been on" is a figure:** **0 of 47** shipped configs set `hil_review` at all — 0.0000%, Wilson [0.0000%, 7.5558%], Clopper-Pearson [0.0000%, 7.5486%] — and **0 archived run reports** carry `hil_status` or `hil_paused_at_round`. The **54 `.py` and 28 `.json`** files under `bench/logs/` that mention those keys are sandbox-harvest COPIES OF THE RUNNER SOURCE. **Counting them would have reported 82 pauses where there were none** — exactly the self-inclusion shape task C2 now guards against, and the producer draws the distinction explicitly.

**WHY THE OWED RUN WAS NOT LAUNCHED 2026-10-17 15:24 BST, measured rather than assumed.** A simulated run is not free in compute: it dispatches 6 stand-in seats as local Claude subagents, and leg 3 of the shakedown took **71.5 minutes for 5 rounds**. With the convergence window now at 7 and `exp56` capped at 8 rounds, a run that converges needs at least 8 rounds, so the wall-clock roughly doubles. Against that, his machine at the time of writing: **8.0 GiB of RAM, 61 MiB free, load average 3.20** with this session's own test runs already on it. The standing record is that this machine is a fixed resource with a budget of 2 heavy processes. **So the run is READY and is his to start when the machine is free**, rather than something to spend his memory on unannounced. Everything the run would exercise has been verified by direct execution in the meantime, and the guards covering it are in the commit-time set.
**HALF BUILT 2026-10-17 15:20 BST; the free-panel check he asked for is the half still owed.** His ruling was *"Fix it and check your fix with the free panel."*

**THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THE DEFAULT AND EVERY CALLER OVERRODE IT.** `RunnerConfig.gamma_alt_consecutive_zero_crit` derives from `REQUIRED_QUIET_RUN = 7`, chosen because the committed measurement at `bench/the_false_quiet_rate_is_not_the_gates_rate_2026-10-08.py` shows the premature-window rate falling **0.1264 → 0.0784 → 0.0458 → 0.0263 → 0.0000** as the window goes 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → 7. But **32 of 47 shipped configs set the value to 3 explicitly**, so the repair was inert for every experiment that matters — an addition nothing reaches.

**ONLY 12 OF THE 32 WERE RAISED, AND 20 WERE DELIBERATELY NOT.** A config is also the RECORD of how an experiment was run. Of the 32, **20 belong to experiments with archived runs** and are left exactly as they are, because editing them would misrepresent how exp40 through exp49, exp53 and exp55 were conducted. The other **12 have no archived run** — exp50 physics, exp51 biology, exp52's 5 factorial cells and exp56's 3 arms — and every one carries `max_rounds` of 8 or 16, so a window of 7 is reachable. Feasibility was checked BEFORE the change: **31 of 33 configs setting the knob have `max_rounds` ≥ 7, 93.9394%**, Wilson [80.3938%, 98.3219%], Clopper-Pearson [79.7736%, 99.2574%].

**THE COST, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED.** A window of 7 needs at least 7 settled rounds, so a converging run takes 8 rounds where it took 4. The 3 shakedown legs converged at rounds 3, 4 and 4. **On a PAID run that is roughly double the dispatches**, and it is the price of taking the premature-window rate from 0.1264 to 0.0000. If that trade is not the one he intended, this is the number to say so against.

**A HISTORICAL FINDING FELL OUT OF THE FEASIBILITY CHECK.** `bench/exp42_configs/42_composer_confirm.json` sets a window of 3 inside `max_rounds` of 2, so the settled-round convergence route could never have been satisfied in that run and it must have terminated by another path. It is archived, so it is left alone and PINNED by a guard instead, which refuses if someone later tidies it and erases the fact.

**WHAT IS STILL OWED: the free-panel check.** Both directions are guarded — raising an archived config fails, leaving an unrun one at 3 fails — but his ruling names a panel check and that has not been run.

K=7 is the first window with ZERO premature closes observed and the smallest whose upper bound meets the target. Going further does not help and eventually hurts: by **K=9 the upper bound has risen back to 0.050651, above the target**, because the denominator shrinks as the window lengthens. The 3 shakedown legs converged at rounds 3, 4 and 4 under K=3; under 7 none could close before round 7, so a converging run grows by about 4 rounds — on leg 3's measured 71.5 minutes over 5 rounds, roughly an hour more per run and 4 more rounds of paid dispatch. **[NEEDS YOUR RULING] if that is too expensive:** K=5 halves the added cost and still meets the target on the point estimate, at an upper bound of 0.096324. **BOTH GATES READ IT, verified rather than assumed:** the legacy quiet arm and condition (C) of the hardened conjunction gate, which C8 made the default the same morning — so this is not inert under either path. The window is now a parameter defaulting to 3, the length the claim was measured at, and the gate is handed a configuration whose quiet run EQUALS the window — otherwise the count arm refuses and the gamma arm is never reached, which is a different question entirely. The historical claim reproduces exactly: 23 of 23 windows, the gate fired on 1.0 of them, and gamma blocked none.

A **completion marker** separates *printed* from *not printed*, and a DEAD seat never prints it either — so it cannot tell finished from died. **The clock separates nothing:** measured by `scripts/the_cap_was_measured_on_censored_data_2026-10-08.py`, a degraded attempt issued **88 tool calls at 20.455 s per call and never converged**, a healthy one **converged in 49 at 20.816 s per call**, and a third seat ran at 15.024 s per call — a spread of **5.792 s** across all 3. The RATE is stable whether a seat is working or churning; only the COUNT differs, so a longer clock buys churn, which is why the dispatcher's record of a 3600 s raise reverted within the hour stands. **A live call count separates all 3 states** — working, churning, dead. **AND WHY IT HAD TO BE BUILT BEFORE THE CAP COULD STOP BEING ARBITRARY.** All **135** archived per-attempt records carry a total count and a total elapsed, and **not one carries a time per call**, so the inter-call gap distribution — the only thing a non-arbitrary stall bound can be derived from — is unmeasurable today. A count threshold alone would not do it either: the 2 attempts killed at the cap had **78 and 89** calls, both below the 90th percentile of 95, so they were killed by TIME at a normal call count. This change produces the missing data; the derived bound is the second step and is NOT claimed as done. **WHAT IS BUILT.** `_run_cli_incrementally` consumes the CLI's stdout as it arrives, so (1) the tool-call count is observable **mid-flight** and logged every 10th call, where before no progress signal existed at all; (2) **partial output survives a timeout** — `subprocess.run(capture_output=True)` discards everything when it raises and the old handler never read `e.output`, which is the recorded **"1800 s for 0 words"**; and (3) every call is **stamped with its arrival time**. **STRICTLY ADDITIVE.** It runs only where a tool-log sink is set, which is already the only place the CLI is asked for stream-json, so the text path keeps `subprocess.run` untouched and a dispatch without a sink behaves exactly as before — pinned by a test that parses the dispatcher to require both calls present. The timeout still raises the same exception type, so the retry path is unchanged. **ONE PARSING RULE, NOT TWO.** The per-line parse was factored out of `_parse_stream_json` so the incremental reader and the whole-capture parser cannot drift — the shape the note linter's own `paragraphs()` records breaking an exemption set. The caller then compares the 2 counts over the same bytes and REPORTS a disagreement rather than silently preferring one. **GUARDS:** 9 tests, 5 of 5 mutations caught — dropping the partial output from the timeout, removing the timestamps, never calling the progress hook, letting a hook exception kill a dispatch, and re-spelling the per-line rule (which fails 7 of 9). **AND 2 DEFECTS OF MINE WERE FOUND WHILE DOING IT.** My earlier counter refactor left an **unreachable `return len(blocking)`** after the real return, referring to a name that no longer exists in that scope — dead, and it would have raised `NameError` had anything reached it. And `test_a_deferred_critical_can_stop_blocking_A4_eventually` was asserting on the SOURCE TEXT of `unverified_critical_count`, searching for a string the refactor had moved into `blocking_entry_ids`; the behaviour never changed, but a source-text test cannot tell a behaviour change from a rename. It now builds a registry, marks a critical exhausted, and requires the counter to stop counting it — and requires the sibling routing reader to agree, since one predicate was the point. Disabling the valve turns it red.

**BUILT 2026-10-17 16:02 BST. THE PANEL'S FINAL TAKE IS THE HALF STILL OWED, which is the half he asked about.** His question was *"Didn't you say all you had to do was build what had already been recommended and that you would put this to the panel for their final take on your design? Why didn't you do that yet?"* The build is now done and the panel step needs a dispatch his machine cannot take at present; it is recorded beside the owed runs on C12 and C15 with the same resource figures.

**WHAT IS BUILT, and it is wired rather than offered.** `bench/routing.py` now carries `capability_identity(provider, model_identifier, version)`, a **stable 12-character digest** over the normalised triple, with `capability_provenance()` carrying the readable inputs for a reader and deciding nothing — his design as put to the free panel on 2026-10-14, verbatim: *"Key every capability decision on a STABLE IDENTITY — a hash over (provider, model identifier, version) — with the vendor name recorded as PROVENANCE and deciding nothing."* `falsification_evidence_by_identity()` pools each seat's refuted-of-total pair onto the identity it resolves to, through the registry's own `resolve_model`, and `season_strength_order()` now **ranks IDENTITIES and orders the seats by the rank of the identity each carries**. So 2 seats of one model share a position instead of being ranked against each other — the same reasoning the refutation tally already applies when counting distinct models.

**WHY A DIGEST RATHER THAN THE READABLE TRIPLE.** The runner already held `registry.model_identity`, mapping a seat to `model_id@api`. That is an identity, but it is a NAME, and a name invites a capability decision keyed on it — the practice he has rejected 4 times. A digest cannot be read as a vendor, so a ranking keyed on it cannot smuggle one in. A stand-in seat shares the identity of the model it stands in for, which under-separates deliberately: under-separation makes auto-refutation HARDER, which is the direction that preserves findings. An empty identifier is REFUSED rather than hashed, because an identity derived from nothing is stable, shared by every such model, and silently wrong.

**THE MEASURED GAIN, by execution rather than by argument.** Two seats of one model against one seat of another, with falsification outcomes of **2 refuted of 2 and 2 of 2 against 0 of 4**: ranked PER SEAT it does **not** establish and falls back to the vendor tuple; ranked PER IDENTITY it **does** establish and returns a measured order. Pooling both raises the sample per identity and cuts the comparisons Holm must correct for — **1 pair instead of 3** — so a measurement survives where it previously did not. **And it does not fabricate:** with 2 of 2, 1 of 2 and 0 of 4 it still refuses and still returns the recorded fallback.

**HIS "LOUD, NEVER DEFAULTED" CLAUSE IS NOW HONOURED.** An identity carrying no falsification evidence is NAMED in the reason, with its digest, so a roster with no evidence reads differently from one whose evidence failed to separate. Before this both produced the same sentence.

**WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: retiring `DEFAULT_FALSIFIER_STRENGTH`**, the other half of the panel design. Emptying it collides with his earlier **2026-10-07** ruling about ladder MEMBERSHIP — measured earlier today, it breaks 8 guards that implement that ruling — and the collision is already recorded for him beside the constant. The fallback therefore still exists, and the gain above is that **it is reached less often**.

It is not 2 pieces of work. **★ ITEM (3) IS NOW ADOPTED, 2026-10-29T03:53:49+00:00, AND ADOPTING IT MEANT MAKING IT CHECKABLE RATHER THAN CHANGING ANY BEHAVIOUR.** **BOTH PROPERTIES ALREADY HELD BY CONSTRUCTION**, which the FOLLOW step established before any code was written: `falsification_evidence_from_registry` reduces each seat to a `(refuted, total)` PAIR, `falsification_evidence_by_identity` pools those pairs, and `strength_order_from_evidence` ranks on the pairs alone -- so no permutation of the underlying outcomes can reach the ranking, and ties break on the seat label rather than on dict iteration order. **I NEARLY REPORTED AN EXCHANGEABILITY VIOLATION THAT DOES NOT EXIST:** `bench/routing.py:70` reads *"a seat needs 12 consecutive successes"*, and *consecutive* looks order-dependent. It is not -- it describes the ARITHMETIC of 12 of 12 attempts on a Wilson lower bound, which is a function of k and n only. That was the 6th false start caught tonight by following before building. **AN INVARIANT THAT HOLDS ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION IS ONE A REFACTOR CAN REMOVE WITH NOTHING OBJECTING**, and no guard held either property, so the adoption is the guard: `bench/tests/test_the_ranking_is_deterministic_and_exchangeable_2026-10-29.py`, 9 tests, which EXECUTE the real functions -- repeated calls agreeing exactly, every permutation of the evidence dict giving one order, 2 registries with the same per-seat counts but the refusals moved to the END of each run ranking identically, the entry order reversed changing nothing, and the season freeze surviving contradicting evidence. **AND MY FIRST VERSION OF THAT GUARD HAD NO TEETH, WHICH IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE USEFUL PART.** 2 mutations of the live sort -- deleting the stable label tiebreak, and ranking on the RAW refutation count instead of the rate -- both left it GREEN. 2 fixture defects, each the same shape: the tie test used `{"A": (5, 10), "B": (5, 10)}` with nothing else, and that evidence SEPARATES NO PAIR, so the function returns an empty order BEFORE the sort and the test compared `()` with `()`; and every fixture used `total=30` for every seat, so rate-order and count-order COINCIDED and the productivity mutation was invisible. Repaired with a 3rd separating seat so the tie is reachable, and with unequal totals -- A refuting 10 of 10 against B refuting 20 of 40, where A leads on rate and B on raw count -- so the 2 orders disagree. **Both mutations now turn it RED and the restored tree is green, with `bench/routing.py` verified byte-identical to its pre-mutation state.** Ranking on the count is the PRODUCTIVITY inversion a free seat refuted on 2026-10-06, so that second mutation is not hypothetical. **WHAT REMAINS GENUINELY OPEN HERE IS 3 ITEMS, not 6:** (2) the cold-start draw -- **AND THE RESEARCH THAT BEARS ON IT IS ALREADY COMMITTED AND WAS NOT REFLECTED HERE, which is a coverage defect found 2026-10-29T04:03:29+00:00 and is the 9th thing tonight that existed before I looked for it.** The file is `experimental_notes/evidence/allocation_prior_art_2026-10-09/research_online_learning_for_cold_start_identification_of_the.json`, 1 of **6** research dimensions committed that day. **ITS VERDICT ON HIS DESIGN: the wheel is invented 3 times over, and 1 of the 3 is his exact design already built, measured and published.** (a) *"assess the best, recurse on the rest"* IS **Sequential Halving** -- Karnin, Koren and Somekh, *Almost Optimal Exploration in Multi-Armed Bandits*, ICML 2013, PMLR 28:1238-1246, Algorithm 2 -- ported to hyperparameter selection by Jamieson and Talwalkar (AISTATS 2016, PMLR 51:240-248) and parallelised as ASHA (Li et al., MLSys 2020, arXiv:1810.05934). (b) *"hard item first, easier item if you fail, recurse"* IS **Computerized Adaptive Testing** on Item Response Theory: Lord's self-scoring flexilevel test (1971), Weiss's stratified-adaptive model (1973) which bins the bank into **9 difficulty strata -- his tiers** -- and Birnbaum's 2PL (1968) supplying the maximum-Fisher-information selection rule. Waters (1975) measured stradaptive testing at 19.2, 26.5 and 31.5 items per subject against 48.4 conventional: **about a 2 times saving, not 100 times.** **THE ONE STRUCTURAL OBJECTION TO HIS DESIGN AS SPECIFIED:** halving varies BUDGET across rounds and holds the instrument fixed, whereas he varies DIFFICULTY and holds the budget at 1 -- and **varying difficulty without an equating model makes round-1 and round-2 scores incommensurable, so no single total order exists over the roster.** IRT is the equating machinery that would be required. **AND HIS "2 OR 3 ATTEMPTS" IS REFUTED BY EXACT ARITHMETIC, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE IT WAS HIS ANSWER TO THE 300-ATTEMPT FIGURE HE RIGHTLY REJECTED.** On exact binomial over rationals: **1 attempt orders a 0.70-success model above a 0.50-success model with probability 3/5 = 0.600**, barely above a coin flip, and 3 attempts give **1371/2000 = 0.6855**. For 95% correct ordering of that single pair: **32 attempts each**; at gap 0.10, **134**; at gap 0.05, **540**. scipy's binomial and the normal approximation agree within 2 trials at every gap tested. **WORSE, HIS OWN ROSTER-SCALE CONSTRAINT FORBIDS HIS OWN BUDGET:** Sequential Halving's round-0 allocation is `t_0 = T / (n * ceil(log2 n))`, so at T = 3n the first round gets **0.429 pulls per arm at n = 70 and 0.300 at n = 700** -- below 1 -- so Sequential Halving cannot run at 3 attempts per model for the 70-or-700-model case he asked it to cope with. **HE IS NONETHELESS RIGHT THAT 300 UNIFORMLY IS WASTEFUL:** at gaps of 0.30 to 0.50, **14 to 32 attempts suffice**, and coarse tiering at epsilon = 0.25 needs **48**. So the 300 figure was over-stated and 3 is under-stated, and the defensible region is tens rather than either. **3 THINGS IN HIS DESIGN ARE GENUINELY UNSOLVED IN THE LITERATURE, WHICH IS A FINDING IN HIS FAVOUR AND NOT AGAINST IT.** First, **no published method combines fixed-confidence best-arm identification WITH per-arm degradation detection AND re-admission of previously eliminated arms** -- and re-admission is exactly his NO-BENCHING rule, that *"a loser keeps its position rather than being knocked out"*. ASHA's rolling base-rung admission is the closest existing thing. Second, **common-mode degradation breaks the standard stopping rule**, and this is the SAME degeneracy as the relegation-identifiability result above: Zierahn et al., *Best Arm Identification for Bandits with Shifting Means*, arXiv:2610.10488v1, Lemma 9, shows that under an unobserved common shift the generalised-likelihood-ratio statistic *"is uniformly 0 at all times, irrespective of the observed data"* -- so a GLRT that models the shift never stops and one that ignores it is not delta-correct, and Track-and-Stop inherits the failure. Third, **fully decentralised best-arm identification with no coordinator is not solved**: Hillel et al. (NeurIPS 2013) show 1 round of communication buys a sqrt(k) speedup and prove it optimal for that regime up to constant factors. **SO ITEM (2) IS NOT "BUILD THE DRAW". It is: adopt Sequential Halving's skeleton, decide whether to carry an IRT equating model or to hold difficulty fixed and vary budget as the proven form does, and set the number of attempts per model in the tens on the measured gaps rather than at 3 or at 300.** **★ ITEM (5) IS ANSWERED, 2026-10-29T03:57:59+00:00, AND THE ANSWER IS YES: THE POOL EXISTS AND WAS BUILT ON HIS OWN RULING. THAT IS A 7th NEAR-REBUILD CAUGHT BY FOLLOWING FIRST.** `bench/lean_anchor_2026-10-11.py` is *"a difficulty-INVARIANT anchor set, graded by the Lean kernel rather than by a judge"*, built on his ruling of 2026-10-11: *"y, build the anchor set with Lean, and let's utilise it (minus the hype) appropriately in our work going forward"*, with the condition *"if it adds nothing of use, then there is no requirement for you to do it"*. **IT ANSWERS THE DEGENERACY CORRECTLY AND FOR THE RIGHT REASON.** The anchor does NOT carry a difficulty RATING, and its own header says why: a rating measured from our own models would be circular, which is the same degeneracy again. What it needs is difficulty to be INVARIANT, and the pool is invariant on 2 counts -- the statements are byte-stable committed data, and the grader is a deterministic proof kernel rather than a model judging another model. In its words: *"If a seat's pass count on this pool falls, the work did not get harder -- it is literally the same bytes checked by the same kernel."* It also already refuses the 4 ways a Lean proof passes for the wrong reason -- `sorry`, `axiom`, `native_decide` and RESTATEMENT -- each measured on this machine before the file was written, with restatement named as the check the pool's invariance rests on, because the literature reports formal-versus-informal discrepancies in more than half of miniF2F. **SO THE PRECONDITION IS SATISFIABLE AND THE DECISION IS MADE. 2 CONCRETE RESIDUALS REPLACE IT, BOTH MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** **FIRST, THE POOL IS NOT REACHED BY THE MECHANISM IT EXISTS TO ENABLE.** `lean_anchor` is imported by exactly 2 files: itself and `bench/tests/test_the_lean_anchor_refuses_the_loopholes_2026-10-11.py`. Nothing in the routing, ranking or relegation path consults it. It is executed by a test, so it is not an unwired addition in the letter of the additive standard, but its PURPOSE is unreached: relegation remains unspecified in practice even though its precondition is now available. **SECOND, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT BEARS ON HIS "ADDS NOTHING OF USE" CONDITION: AT 5 ITEMS THE POOL CAN SEE A COLLAPSE AND NOTHING SUBTLER.** `POOL` holds 5 statements, L1 to L5. Treating a seat's 2 occasions as paired and testing the discordant items by an exact sign test: a fall from **5 of 5 to 0 of 5 gives p = 0.031250**, which clears 0.05; **5 to 1 gives 0.062500**, which does not; **5 to 3 gives 0.250000**; and **5 to 4 gives 0.500000**. Cross-checked with Fisher one-sided on the same tables, which is more permissive at 0.003968, 0.023810, 0.222222 and 0.500000, and with Clopper-Pearson on the later rate. **A 1-IN-5 DEGRADATION NEEDS ABOUT 30 ITEMS** to clear 0.05 on the sign test: at n = 20 the 6-discordant equivalent gives 0.062500 and at **n = 30 it gives 0.015625**. Since relegation is for SUSTAINED DEGRADATION rather than collapse, the pool is sound in design and roughly 6 times too small in size. **AND A CURRENCY POINT WORTH 1 LINE:** the statements were compiled against **Lean 4.34.1** and this machine now runs **4.35.0**, so whether they still check is a question its guard answers rather than one this entry should assume. (5) SUPERSEDED by the 2 residuals above -- grow the pool to about 30 items, and wire it to the relegation decision; and **(6) WHICH IS SUBSTANTIALLY DONE AND HE HAS ALREADY APPROVED ITS DEFERRAL, established 2026-10-29T04:02:15+00:00 -- the 8th near-rebuild caught tonight by reading the record first.** **BOTH HALVES WERE DELIVERED ON 2026-10-09.** The archaeology is in `experimental_notes/Morning_Report_2026-10-09.md`, which opens *"You asked whether the early discussions of distributed and peer-to-peer operation went anywhere"*, and the external research is COMMITTED as evidence at `experimental_notes/evidence/allocation_prior_art_2026-10-09/`, which carries `archaeology_distributed_p2p_history.json`, `research_peer_to_peer_coordination_with_no_central_server_and.json` and a `verify_` companion. **WHAT IT FOUND, AND IT CONTRADICTS THE PREMISE RATHER THAN SUPPORTING IT.** A note of 19 March 2026 established that *"distributed compute"* in this project means EPISTEMIC DIVERSITY rather than network distribution. A specification of 26 August, 141 lines, names Folding@home, Rosetta@home, SETI@home and Einstein@home in its opening paragraph, and its own closing line reads *"Nothing described here has been built."* Its finding: a distributed version recruiting 50 architectures to review 1 artefact is **a distributed system doing the work of about 4**, so panel size should be FIXED at 4 to 6 and not scaled -- re-derived from the live archive by a guard of 10 tests. The reason is the project's own coverage model: when the correlation between architectures reaches 1, adding architectures adds nothing. **AND THE VOLUNTEER-COMPUTING SHAPE DOES NOT TRANSFER, for a reason that goes to this project's premise:** those projects solve many untrusted machines doing IDENTICAL work and resolve disagreement BY MAJORITY, whereas here disagreement IS the signal, and the specification says the project has no equivalent of a quorum step. **WHAT WAS BUILT: nothing for peer-to-peer.** Networking code appears in exactly 4 files and all 4 use network calls to FORBID network access during tests. 3 things do exist: a topology switch offering a star or a relay, where the relay is still a central relay and therefore still a coordinator; an allocation module shelved in August by his own ruling, never having run outside its own tests; and 1 genuinely valuable component dated 5 April, a verification chain providing content-addressed digests, detached signatures and Merkle trees with inclusion proofs, which is live and tested and is real substrate for a system with no central authority. **THE CLEAREST BLOCKER IS NAMED:** the finding registry's identity scheme, since findings are named by a COUNTER, so 2 peers would both mint the same identifier and there is no merge rule -- and the fix, content-addressed identity, is something the verification chain already knows how to compute. **HE HAS SEEN AND APPROVED WHERE IT SITS:** deferred past the second bench run, item 36 of the decisions put to him on 6 September with the recommendation to defer, and 1 of 11 items declared excluded from this list. **1 RESIDUAL THE REPORT NAMED WAS STILL UNFIXED 20 DAYS LATER, AND IS FIXED NOW.** It recorded that *"The clearest statement of the peer-to-peer trajectory sits in a file on your Desktop dated 10 April that was never copied into the repository, which is itself worth fixing."* That file is `Perplexity_Revised_Assessment_2026-04-10.txt`, an EXTERNAL assessment, and a search of the FULL git history returns **0 commits** touching that filename -- so for **202 days** it survived only as an unversioned file on one machine's Desktop. It is now mirrored verbatim to `experimental_notes/Perplexity_Revised_Assessment_2026-04-10.md` with a provenance header, the body byte-identical and the source untouched. The passage the report pointed at is its statement that going from a handful of models on 1 machine to a global peer-to-peer mesh is a very long road -- an OUTSIDE assessment of the trajectory rather than the project's own. Plus his 3 unanswered questions of 2026-10-09, which are his to answer and are not work. **RELATED:** C7 the measured statistic, C15 the ranking number, and the withdrawn C10 and C11.

**TWO OF THE FOUR STRANDS SETTLED 2026-10-17 16:35 BST. ONE IS BUILT, ONE IS MEASURED CLEAN, TWO REMAIN.**

**STRAND 1 — THE SEASON IS 1 ROUND, AND IT IS BUILT.** His design, *"then for the rest of the rounds in an experiment the positions of these models in their rankings stays fixed"*, is `routing.season_strength_order`: handed a frozen order it returns it unchanged, handed none it measures this experiment's own falsification outcomes. It is wired at the runner's `route` call, the order is logged as MEASURED or DEFAULTED with its reason, and task C18 extended it on 2026-10-17 to rank hashed capability IDENTITIES rather than seat labels. **That is the change he said makes promotion and relegation affordable, and it bounds the evidence requirement to 1 round rather than the 300 attempts he rejected.**

**STRAND 2 — THE TERMINOLOGY REJECTION IS HONOURED WHERE IT WAS AIMED, measured rather than asserted.** His words: *"we shouldn't have our code littered with football references... or even 'leagues', as such"*. `scripts/is_the_ladder_littered_with_football_2026-10-17.py` scans by AST, so a comment quoting him is not mistaken for code he objected to: the **ranking mechanism carries 0** of the terms — `bench/routing.py`, `bench/reference_runner_v3.py` and `bench/runner_core.py` name things `strength_order`, `capability_identity` and `season_strength_order`. The 28 other hits in the live harness account for themselves as **9 plus 6 plus 13**: 9 are `ZeroDivisionError`, a Python builtin caught by "division"; **6 are `player` or `player_manager` as a ROLE NAME**, predating the ladder discussion and present inside ARCHIVED PROMPTS, so renaming them would edit the record of runs already performed — **that one is his call, not a tidy-up**; and 13 quote or analyse his own analogy in the 2026-10-08 producers, where renaming would erase what was measured.

**A CRUDE SUBSTRING SCAN WAS TRIED FIRST and is why the producer uses the AST.** It reported **190** hits for "promot", which is this project's own SHADOW PROMOTION; **64** for "division", mostly arithmetic; and **25** for "fixture", which is pytest. A total over a proxy produced a number again.

**STRAND 3 — THE KNOCKOUT COLD START IS NOT BUILT.** His design: all models get 1 hard randomly-generated mathematics problem, ranked by who solves it fastest using tools and writing proofs or falsifiers and most cost-effectively, with weaker performers then given a less complex problem and the step repeated until the ranking settles. That needs model dispatches to exercise and is recorded with the owed runs.

**STRAND 4 — RELEGATION ON SUSTAINED DEGRADATION HAS NO ANSWER AS POSED, and that result stands.** Verified 3 ways: symbolic algebra returning exactly 0, a constraint solver returning unsatisfiable on any separating value, and arbitrary-precision arithmetic returning 0 difference across 27 test points. It is not a defect to fix; it is a statement about what can be identified without an externally-anchored item pool.

★ **C21 WAS WRONG ON ITS CENTRAL CLAIM AND IS CORRECTED, 2026-10-17T03:51:59+01:00, on his exact quote.** C21 presented the up-down-sideways framing as open design work and dated it to his reframing of 2026-10-09. Both are wrong. **He supplied the verbatim original, dated 2026-10-15T03:40:13 in the session transcript:** *"Routing should be blind to what a models name is anyway, it should just look at the available resources and if it can't climb, then it should climb sideways, to the next highest rated available model, even if that is another version of Opus 4.8. It isn't just about capability, its about available resources and using them in the most efficient way possible. Think of it less as a straight 'up and down ladder' and more like a game of snakes and ladders, where the objective is to clear the outstanding workload as efficiently as possible! Side steps (as well and down steps) on such a 'ladder' are fully permitted, according to a measure of both capability and available resources!"* **AND CC1 ANSWERED IT IN FULL 103 SECONDS LATER, at 2026-10-15T03:41:56, conceding the point:** *"Your snakes-and-ladders description is not a change request, it is what the code already does. It takes the available labels, ranks strongest-first, drops the one that already failed, and tries the next, which may be an equal-capability peer. Side-steps are already permitted; routing is already blind to version and keyed on what is available."* **THAT ANSWER IS NOW VERIFIED BY EXECUTION RATHER THAN TRUSTED**, because a prior claim of CC1's is still a claim. `bench/routing.rank_falsifier_writers(labels, strength_order=..., exclude=...)` was called directly: it returns `['CC2', 'Gemini', 'Fable']` when only those 3 are available, so it ranks strongest-first over AVAILABILITY; `exclude` drops the failed label in 3 of 3 cases tried; with `CC2` excluded and a `CC2-SIM` present it still offers `CC2-SIM`, so it is **blind to version** exactly as he specified; and excluding `CC2` from `['CC2','CC2-SIM','Codex-SIM']` leaves `['Codex-SIM','CC2-SIM']`, so a side-step to an equal-capability peer exists. **SO THE MECHANISM IS BUILT, SETTLED, AND MUST NOT BE RAISED AGAIN.** **WHAT THE SAME EXECUTION SHOWS IS GENUINELY OPEN, and it is his C7 objection stated precisely.** `strength_order` defaults to the literal tuple `('Codex', 'CC2', 'ChatGPT', 'Gemini', 'DeepSeek', 'Fable')` — a hard-coded list of vendor names. That is verbatim what he objected to on 2026-10-05: *"The routing ladder should be a measured statistic, not simply a list of named vendors based on what you, or I may have read about them on the internet."* The ORDER is the open item, not the movement. The movement is done. **C15 shares that root**, since a ranking number derived from measured capability is what would replace the vendor tuple. **ALSO SETTLED IN THE SAME EXCHANGE, and recorded here so it is not rediscovered either:** the number of fallback rungs a round may spend, not seat scattering, is what makes the ladder mix — budget 2 gives 0 of 6 mixed, budget 3 gives 3 of 6, budget 4 gives 6 of 6 mixed and 6 of 6 correctly directed, and the shakedown launched at `--routing-max-rungs 0`, exhaust, which is past budget 4. And **Sonnet is ruled out**: he ruled against it on 2026-09-07 — *"They should be Opus Agents! Opus is your dedicated coding platform. Sonnet is its 'chatbot' cousin and significantly less capable"* — and CC1 recommended it anyway on 2026-10-15, which it conceded as resurrecting struck advice. **WHAT REMAINS OF C21 AS WRITTEN:** only the 2026-10-09 division and relegation material, which is a different question from the movement mechanism — the 1-round season with rankings frozen thereafter, the cold-start draw, his rejection of the 300-attempt figure in favour of 2 or 3, stripping football vocabulary, the anchor-set precondition, the external research and the archaeology, and his 3 unanswered questions. Those stand.

**C22. TOP OF THE LIST, ON HIS INSTRUCTION: a ticket store that makes "settled" a recorded field instead of something CC1 infers, harvesting from chat as well as his replies.** Added 2026-10-17T03:54:51+01:00. **HIS INSTRUCTION, 2026-10-17:** *"perhaps you could reuse/repurpose some of our existing machinery from the harness in terms of our Bugzilla machinery, which you could them check mechanically so that you would be compelled to use it, and so that issues like this cannot arise again? Would this work?"* And: *"This Bugzilla fix should probably be placed at the top of your currents list."* And the scope extension: *"you will need to adapt it in such a way, that it can also extract/harvest, track and store any pending fixes and changes you or I propose in chat too, rather than just directly from my tts responses."* **THE MACHINERY HE REMEMBERS EXISTS.** `bench/bugzilla_loop.py`, 898 lines, implements the Bugzilla paradigm from the Experiment 36 design analysis of 7 April 2026, with the finite-state machine `OPEN -> CONFIRMED -> CLOSED` where CLOSED is documented as *"challenge-resistant, terminal"*, `DUPLICATE -> MERGED`, and `CLOSED -> REOPENED` only via *"explicit REOPEN + HIL escalation"*. Its own docstring states that CLOSED findings appear in summaries *"with 'do not re-describe' instruction"*. **That is precisely the set-in-concrete facility he asked for, and it already exists for experiment findings.** What it does not cover is project-level instructions and rulings, which live in a markdown list whose state CC1 infers from prose. **THE EVIDENCE THAT INFERENCE CANNOT WORK, measured on 2026-10-17 and the reason this item is first.** 6 attempts to determine settledness or novelty from text failed in a single night: a word-overlap coverage measure that passed its control and answered the wrong question; a repeatability measure saturated at 90.0000 per cent across every threshold; a lint claim that was a type error returning an empty list; a rediscovery triage that self-matched because the corpus included the task list; a novelty search that reported 0 files because it covered neither the source tree nor the chat transcripts; and a function called with the wrong positional argument. **The common shape is that each produced a clean answer that was an artefact of scope or signature, not a measurement.** A recorded state field has no scope and no signature. **WHAT THE HARVEST MUST COVER, measured.** Of his 579 annotations, **0** mention the orchestrator-in-the-harness question, which he has now asked 3 times and which exists only in chat. So a harvester reading only his replies is blind to it by construction. The transcripts ARE readable: his verbatim 2026-10-15T03:40:13 message and CC1's reply 103 seconds later were both recovered from `a07b3790-...jsonl` by timestamp, so chat harvesting is feasible rather than aspirational. **FOR DONE, 4 parts.** (1) A ticket store whose state field is one of the existing vocabulary, with CLOSED terminal and reopenable only by him. (2) A harvester over both his annotated replies and the session transcripts, which records the verbatim source text and its timestamp so a human read settles identity in seconds. (3) A **Stop**-event gate that refuses to end a turn in which a CLOSED ticket was presented as open — Stop hooks can refuse, and 4 did so correctly on 2026-10-16 and 2026-10-17, while prompt-submit hooks only advise. (4) A duplicate check on the 2 elements he specified: same fault AND same proposed repair. **THE HONEST LIMIT, because he asked whether it can be made unable to lie.** It can make the STATE impossible to misreport, because a state is read rather than inferred. It cannot make IDENTITY impossible to misjudge: deciding that a new finding IS a given CLOSED ticket is the duplicate problem, and tonight shows CC1's text matchers fail at exactly that. The mitigation is that a ticket carries his verbatim words, so a human read resolves it immediately — which is how the routing-ladder rediscovery was caught. **So: state, yes; identity, reduced but not eliminated.**
