**B49. THE LIMIT ON TICKETS CARRYING NO IDENTITY IS IRREVERSIBLE ONCE BREACHED, AND I BREACHED IT: 3 TICKETS I RAISED BY HAND TOOK THE COUNT FROM 317 TO 320 AND NO AVAILABLE REMEDY LOWERS IT.** Added 2026-10-29T08:13:53+00:00. **HOW IT WAS FOUND.** `bench/tests/test_an_identity_is_derived_not_remembered_2026-10-28.py::test_the_identityless_count_does_not_grow` is red in the full suite of 2026-10-29, and it is CORRECT to be red. **320 of 2,887 tickets carry neither a `task_ref` nor an `anchor`**, 0.110842 with a 95% Wilson interval of [0.099903, 0.122814] agreed to 6 decimal places by statsmodels and an independent NumPy closed form against a ceiling of 317 measured at 2026-10-28T02:10:18Z. The 3 additions are `qs:7d558a3e3381`, `pf:b4517f6ecfe6` and `pf:b0d8ca44a450`, all OPEN, all `source=chat`, all created by me between 04:05 and 06:06 on 2026-10-29. **THIS IS THE DOCUMENTED MISTAKE REPEATED.** The escape hatch is `TicketStore.propose_from_chat`, which DERIVES the anchor as `chat#<clock>#<8 hex of the text>`; its own docstring records that on 2026-10-27 5 questions were raised by hand, all 5 came out identityless, 4 had to be re-raised, and that *"the escape hatch `_distinct` provides was designed, documented and simply not used"*. I then did not use it either. **THE 320 CANNOT BE REDUCED, AND THIS IS EXECUTED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** Against a scratch copy of `bench/tickets/tickets.jsonl`: baseline **320**, raise 1 identityless ticket → **321**, call `migrate_identity` onto a tid derived from a proper anchor → **322**, raise the same item correctly through `propose_from_chat` → **322**. It never returned even to the breached level, let alone below it, because a correct re-raise ADDS an anchored record while the bare tombstone stays. The cause is structural and deliberate: `migrate_identity` preserves history by keeping the old record as a `MERGED` tombstone, and that tombstone is itself identityless, so every history-preserving remedy ADDS a bare record. **287 of the 320 are exactly such tombstones**, left by the 2026-10-17 identity migration. **A SECOND DEFECT FOUND IN THE SAME PROBE, AND IT IS INDEPENDENT OF THE RATCHET.** `migrate_identity` copies every field with `asdict(old)` and replaces only `tid`, so migrating onto a tid DERIVED from an anchor produces a record whose `anchor` is still `''` — measured: `moved.anchor == ''` while its tid was computed from `chat#...`. The identifier therefore claims an identity the record does not carry. That is sound for the case the method was built for, where the fields were already right and only the tid was stale, and incoherent for any case where the identity fields are themselves absent. There is no public method that sets `task_ref` or `anchor` in place: of 38 public methods, every mutator is a status transition, `restate`, `retitle` or `migrate_identity`, and none takes an identity field. **A THIRD, SMALLER DEFECT:** `propose_from_chat(when="")` yields the anchor `chat##<hash>`, which identifies no position in the record although locating the item is the single job its docstring gives that field. It is unvalidated. **THE FIX I FIRST PROPOSED IS REFUTED BY ITS OWN BLAST RADIUS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN BUILT.** Making `propose` REFUSE an identityless ticket would close the hole at source and make the ceiling monotonically falling. It would also break a large number of existing tests that raise identityless tickets deliberately, because exercising the subject and word tiers is their entire purpose — **60 call sites across 11 files pass neither field**, led by `test_ticket_store_2026-10-17` with 22, `test_a_shared_mechanism_does_not_decide_2026-10-17` with 8, and `test_the_subject_tier_splits_questions_from_work_2026-10-28` with 6. Those tiers exist FOR identityless items, so forbidding their creation removes the capability they test. **NARROWING THE RATCHET IS ALSO REFUTED, AND BY MEASUREMENT.** The obvious narrowing is to stop counting redirect statuses, which would drop 320 to 33 at a stroke. It is wrong: `MERGED` and `DUPLICATE` are both in `TERMINAL`, and `settled_on_subject` returns every ticket whose status is terminal, documenting a non-empty result as *"DO NOT RAISE"*. So a bare tombstone sharing 1 subject with a genuinely new item can still refuse that item, which is precisely the harm the ratchet names. Excluding redirects would hide a live risk rather than measure it. **WHAT I DID ON MY OWN AUTHORITY, BECAUSE IT IS INDEPENDENT OF THE CEILING.** The 3 tickets are re-raised through `propose_from_chat` with anchors derived from their own `created` stamps, and the original bare records are marked duplicates of them, so each item is retrievable by a real anchor and either identifier leads to it. That fixes retrievability. It does NOT fix the 320, and it is not presented as doing so. **[NEEDS YOUR RULING] THE CEILING IS YOURS TO SET, BECAUSE RE-BASELINING A RATCHET TO ADMIT MY OWN BREACH IS NOT A CHANGE I SHOULD MAKE ALONE.** The 3 options, stated so you can reject all of them: (1) re-baseline to 320 with this entry as the recorded reason, accepting that `migrate_identity` offers no reversal; (2) keep 317 and leave the guard red as a standing reminder, at the cost that a red guard nobody can turn green is the flaky-guard failure this project has already named; (3) give `propose` an identity requirement with an explicit opt-out for the tier tests, which closes the hole but edits 60 call sites across 11 test files and widens the API. My own preference is (1) plus a separate `fr` for an `adopt_identity` method that sets the missing fields and migrates coherently, so that a future breach can be undone — but the ratchet's whole point is that I do not get to move my own goalposts, so it waits for you. Producer for every figure: `scripts/the_identity_ratchet_cannot_be_walked_back_2026-10-29.py`. **A 4TH DEFECT, DEMONSTRATED BY ACCIDENT WHILE REPAIRING THIS ENTRY, 2026-10-29T08:16:22+00:00.** Re-raising the 3 tickets, 2 of them were given `task_ref='B50'` because both concerned that entry. `tid_of` states its rule plainly: *"PRECEDENCE: a task reference wins, then an anchor, then the content"*, and with a reference present the text is EXCLUDED from the digest. So **(kind, task_ref) admits exactly 1 ticket**: both re-raises computed the identical identity `2835f264ae18`, the second write replaced the first at that identity, and `pf:b4517f6ecfe6` was then marked a duplicate of a record carrying a DIFFERENT fault — which fails his condition (a) of 2026-10-16, the same fault, even though the `repair_identity` of 1.0000 was measured honestly against the text present at the moment it was taken. **THIS IS THE DEFECT `tid_of`'S OWN DOCSTRING SAYS THE REFERENCE WAS ADDED TO FIX, RECURRING 1 LEVEL UP.** That docstring records the text-collision case as *"creating a separate ticket for the second overwrote the first in the index -- and the first was CLOSED, so a closed record was destroyed by a later open one. Over-merging hides work; this DELETED it."* Adding the reference to the identity removed the collision between 2 identically-worded ENTRIES; it did not remove the collision between 2 distinct DEFECTS filed against 1 entry with 1 kind. The justification given — that a reference is unique within the list, 141 entries and 141 distinct references — establishes that references do not collide with each other, which is a different claim from the 1 the design needs. **NO OPERATION REPAIRS IT, WHICH IS THIS ENTRY'S ORIGINAL POINT ARRIVING FROM A NEW DIRECTION.** `invalidate` means *"this was never a defect"* and would mislabel a real one; `migrate_identity` carries `task_ref` across unchanged, so the mislabel survives the move and the moved record's identity still would not match its fields; and `reopen` is his alone. **WHAT WAS DONE, AND WHAT WAS LEFT ALONE.** The immune-memory item was re-raised at an ANCHOR-ONLY identity, `pf:b276e059857b`, so each of the 3 items now has exactly 1 live correctly-identified ticket: `pf:b276e059857b` for the immune-memory pins, `pf:2835f264ae18` for the assertion in `test_a_ruled_item_is_not_a_decision_2026-10-16.py`, and `qs:9bc696a93ed1` for the DEFERRED-marker question. Nothing was invalidated, because every one of the 3 describes a real defect. `pf:2835f264ae18` still carries `task_ref=B50` although its subject is that ruled-item assertion rather than the immune memory, and `pf:b4517f6ecfe6` still points at it as a duplicate: both are wrong, both are HISTORY in an append-only register that does not rewrite history, and both are recorded here rather than tidied away. **ADDED TO THE RULING ASKED FOR ABOVE:** whether `tid_of` should admit more than 1 ticket per (kind, task_ref) — by folding the anchor in beneath the reference rather than treating them as alternatives — is a 4th option on the same decision, and it would have prevented this without touching the ceiling at all. **HIS RULING, 2026-10-29**, annotating that recommendation in `Responses/Decisions_Awaiting_You_2026-10-29.txt` and read back through `scripts/what_was_he_annotating_2026-10-29.py`: *"Do the re-baseline and implement your fix as you suggest. fr"* **AND BUILDING THE FIX REFUTED THE PREMISE HIS RULING RESTED ON, which is recorded here because he approved a number that turns out to be wrong.** The entry told him the count could not be brought back down and asked him to re-baseline the ceiling to 320. `TicketStore.adopt_identity`, built on his ruling, lowered it: it succeeded on 1 of the 3 offending tickets and was correctly refused on the other 2, each of which already carries a correctly identified twin, so inventing a different identity for those would have satisfied the counter rather than the property. **THE FLOOR IS 319, NOT THE 320 HE APPROVED**, and 319 is strictly tighter than what he approved, so no new ruling is owed. Guard: `bench/tests/test_an_identity_can_be_walked_back_2026-10-29.py`, 18 tests, 4 of 4 mutations caught. The 4th option, letting an identity admit more than 1 ticket per (kind, task_ref), remains open and is the identity-collapse decision still listed for him.

**B50. 6 IMMUNE-MEMORY ASSERTIONS NOW FAIL PERMANENTLY, AND THEY ARE ACCUSING A RESET HE HIMSELF ORDERED.** Added 2026-10-29T08:13:53+00:00. **THE 6, from the full suite of 2026-10-29:** `test_immune_memory_evaluation.py::TestWhatTheMemoryHolds::test_disk_memory_reproduces_from_its_recorded_run_reports`, `::test_confirmation_rates_are_a_terminal_decision_ratio_not_a_flaw_rate`, `::TestCounterfactual::test_only_flaw_class_8_can_bind_today_and_it_rests_on_one_observation`, `::TestIntegrityGuards::test_the_live_memory_predates_the_ledger_and_that_is_now_visible`, `::test_the_live_memory_file_still_loads`, and `::TestAdvisoryOnlyUnderAttack::test_but_memory_can_change_a_pipeline_outcome_and_here_is_the_case`. This is the content of ticket `pf:b4517f6ecfe6`, raised 2026-10-29T05:41:17Z and until now recorded without a cause. **THE PRODUCT IS CORRECT AND THE ARCHIVE PROVES IT.** `bench/state/immune_memory.json` holds **1** experiment, `free_run3_no_spec_20261028T235529Z`, written 2026-10-29T00:57:29. The write path in `bench/reference_runner_v3.py:21602` calls `ImmuneMemory.load` BEFORE `record_experiment`, so it accumulates rather than replaces, and a count of 1 is the correct result of accumulating onto an empty store. The store was empty because it was deliberately emptied: `bench/state/immune_memory_archive/immune_memory_20261017T095236Z.why.json` records the reason verbatim as *"his ruling of 2026-10-16: reset immune memory for the upcoming live experiments, since simulation can only tell us so much about how this facility will function in real live/paid experiments"*, together with the archived sha, `experiment_count: 6`, the experiment ids it held, and a named `restore_with` command. **Nothing was lost and nothing is broken.** **WHY THEY FAIL NOW AND NOT ON 2026-10-17, WHICH IS THE INSTRUCTIVE PART.** `reset_record()` was added that day for exactly this false alarm, and its own docstring states the condition: *"IT IS NOT AN EXEMPTION. The condition is '0 records on disk AND an archive exists'. The moment a run writes a record the condition fails and every assertion below applies again, so the suite re-arms itself rather than staying quiet."* free_run3 wrote the first post-reset records on 2026-10-29 at 00:57, so `live._records` became non-empty, `reset_record()` correctly returned None, `_require_populated_memory()` correctly stopped skipping, and the 6 assertions correctly re-armed. **The re-arm is sound design and was built to prevent a permanent exemption. What it re-armed is stale.** The assertions describe a memory populated by `free_run1_directive_whole_20261015T061123Z`, `free_run2_severity_spec_20261015T073418Z` and `leg3_final_shakedown`, whose priors are now in the archive and will never return to the live store. So these 6 will fail on every run from now until the assertions are re-expressed: the same irreversible shape as B49, reached from the opposite direction. **THE 6 ARE NOT 1 PROBLEM, AND THAT IS WHY THIS NEEDS A DECISION RATHER THAN A REWRITE.** **2 of them are a stale CONSTANT and are mechanically fixable.** `_RECORDING_RUNS` at lines 105 to 107 names `exp47_divergence_locationkey_live`, `exp48_chemistry_exam_live` and `exp49_engineering_exam_live` as pre-ledger runs the live memory cannot name itself, and line 291 prepends all 3 unconditionally. Those runs are dated 2026-07-28 and 2026-07-29, **about 3 months BEFORE the reset of 2026-10-17**, so their contributions sit in the archive; prepending them makes the derivable set 4 against a live count of 1, which is precisely the reported `assert 4 == 1`. Filtering the constant to runs that wrote after the newest reset repairs both. **The other 4 are EMPIRICAL CLAIMS about data the reset destroyed, and no filter repairs them.** They assert that some flaw class carries refutation weight, that flaw class 8 produces a positive S\*, that the live memory file loads with its provenance incomplete, and that a constructed memory overrides a uniform prior at R = 0.5000. Each was measured on the pre-reset population. On free_run3 alone the measurements are 0, and reporting that as *"falsifiers have stopped reaching a refutation — the confinement defect of 2026-10-15, or one like it, is back"* is the identical false alarm `reset_record()` was written to stop, now arriving through the re-arm instead of through the absence of a check. **[NEEDS YOUR RULING] WHAT THOSE 4 SHOULD DO AFTER A RESET YOU ORDERED.** The options, stated so you can reject all of them: (1) widen the skip condition from *"0 records and an archive exists"* to *"the live memory no longer contains the runs this claim was measured on"*, which keeps the anti-permanent-exemption intent because it re-arms once comparable runs exist, and costs a more complex predicate; (2) re-measure all 4 against the post-reset memory as it fills, accepting that they assert nothing until enough live runs have accumulated; (3) restore the archived priors with the recorded `restore_with` command, which would make the 4 pass immediately and would also undo the reset you ordered, so it is listed only for completeness and is not recommended. **NOTHING IS BEING CHANGED IN THIS ENTRY.** The 2 mechanically fixable assertions are left alone deliberately, because repairing 2 of 6 would turn a clear single cause into a partial pass that hides the remaining 4 behind a smaller number. Producer for the dates, the archive contents and the write-path ordering: `scripts/the_reset_rearmed_six_stale_pins_2026-10-29.py`.

**THE INVENTORY HIS CAVEAT REQUIRES IS BUILT AND IT COMES BACK CLEAN.** His words: *"We keep all the useful lessons we have learned from the simulated runs and apply them. But we exclude things that only apply in simulated runs."* `scripts/what_is_simulation_only_and_what_carries_forward_2026-10-17.py` measures it by AST, so a comment or a docstring cannot be mistaken for code: **5 of 5 live branches on `run_is_simulated` are CAVEAT and 0 are BEHAVIOUR**, each keyed off the `-SIM` suffix so a paid run turns them off by having no such seat; **6 `-SIM` string literals in the live path**, of which 5 are the suffix constant used to strip a label to its vendor base and 1 is a log message; and the stand-in fingerprints cannot inform a paid run, because `fingerprint_scope` defaults to `experiment` and returns **0 inherited observations against 11 under `persist`, 6 of them stand-ins**. **So the exclusion is STRUCTURAL rather than something to remember, and the lessons carry forward because they were never conditional on simulation.** Guard: `bench/tests/test_nothing_simulation_only_reaches_a_paid_run_2026-10-17.py`, which plants a branch that changes a decision on simulation and requires it to be reported.

**RULED 2026-10-12, AND THE RULING CARRIES A CAVEAT THAT IS ITSELF WORK.** Verbatim: *"Run it next after the outstanding simulated run. But take care that the caveats in place for simulated runs that do not and should not apply to real/paid runs carry over into this run. We keep all the useful lessons we have learned from the simulated runs and apply them. But we exclude things that only apply in simulated runs."*

**SO THIS IS NO LONGER A DECISION; IT IS 2 PIECES OF WORK.** First, the run itself, after the outstanding simulated run. Second, and BEFORE it, an inventory separating settings that must CARRY FORWARD from settings that are SIMULATION ONLY. He asked for that inventory once before, on 2026-08-31, in these words: *"I would also suggest taking an inventory of things in our latest runner that should not be carried forward to our real experiments, like potentially accidentally leaving all routes in the schema pointing at agents rather than real models."* It was never produced. The concrete hazard he names -- every route pointing at a stand-in agent rather than a real model -- would make a paid run measure the simulation, at full cost. Known sim-only settings to start from: the `api="sim"` route on every seat, `fix_efficacy_mode="record"` set by `bench/tools/run_simulated_experiment.py`, and the `-SIM` seat labels. **For DONE:** the inventory exists as a committed artefact, a guard executes it against the live runner, and the Exp 53 launch reads it.
